Row(tuple):\n231.     \"\"\"\n232.     A row in :class:`DataFrame`.\n233.     The fields in it can be accessed:\n234. \n235.     * like attributes (``row.key``)\n236.     * like dictionary values (``row[key]``)\n237. \n238.     ``key in row`` will search through row keys.\n239. \n240.     Row can be used to create a row object by using named arguments.\n241.     It is not allowed to omit a named argument to represent that the value is\n242.     None or missing. This should be explicitly set to None in this case.\n243. \n244.     .. versionchanged:: 3.0.0\n245.         Rows created from named arguments no longer have\n246.         field names sorted alphabetically and will be ordered in the position as\n247.         entered.\n248. \n249.     Examples\n250.     --------\n251.     >>> from pyspark.sql import Row\n252.     >>> row = Row(name=\"Alice\", age=11)\n253.     >>> row\n254.     Row(name='Alice', age=11)\n255.     >>> row['name'], row['age']\n256.     ('Alice', 11)\n257.     >>> row.name, row.age\n258.     ('Alice', 11)\n259.     >>> 'name' in row\n260.
